First query of a fresh service, Vulkan cache off, Metal warm (`tier_latency.sh`: one service per query, specialization off, 300,000 rows; tiers / `PGSTROM_GPUSERV_TIERS=full`): int aggregate 62 ms / 1,021 ms; float8 aggregate 167 ms / 1,009 ms (gpuagg16_4_nogeom 884,196 B created in 0.13 s, against 5,296,152 B in 0.98 s); numeric aggregate 181 ms / 1,029 ms; int join aggregate 225 ms / 4,684 ms (lite scan + lite jagg); float8 join aggregate (a join qual that mixes both sides: the float8 group is in the join program) 961 ms / 3,687 ms (jagg16_4_nogeom 3,014,420 B in 0.91 s, against 9,675,112 B in 3.63 s). A float8 qual on one side of a join is evaluated by the scan kernel of that side and leaves the join kernel on the lite tier. The script prints the pipelines each service created (`PGSTROM_GPUSERV_PIPELINE_LOG=1`), which shows the tier a query used. **Caveat:** these are the warm-Metal figures; the cold ones are the compile times above, and the persisted Vulkan pipeline cache (`~/.cache/pgstrom/pipeline-*.bin`) was at 767 MB of its 768 MB limit (`PGSTROM_GPUSERV_PIPELINE_CACHE_MAX_MB`) when this was measured, so new kernels were no longer being saved: delete the file or raise the limit. Cost: 62 more `slangc` runs per edit of `kernels_vm/` (the nogeom kernels build in seconds each) and `prewarm_kernels.sh` also warms the `_nogeom` set.
- **Why a cold full kernel takes 5 minutes (measured 2026-11-07, `kernel_compile_time.sh`, `pcache_check`).** Pipeline creation of a never-compiled full Arrow GROUP BY-over-join kernel (`ajgagg`) takes 294-329 s whatever the aggregate count (4, 8, 12), group slots (64, 128) or registers (16, 32); the 36-37 s seen first for some variants were Metal-cache hits. The same kernel in the `nogeom` tier: 3.0 MB of SPIR-V and 23.6 s (13x faster); the geometry soft-float code is about 70% of the full kernel. Slang's default `-O1` inlines the interpreter into one 9.6 MB function (717k lines of SPIR-V assembly); `[noinline]` on the `vm_geom*` functions changes nothing (the interpreter `vm_run` is itself inlined at about six call sites in `jgagg_main` and `arrow_eval_row`, and Metal inlines whatever is left). `-O0` gives 430 KB / 114 functions but Metal still needs 257 s (it inlines them again), and `-O0` needs the local `half` of `f64_soft.inc` renamed (a Metal keyword). So outlining on our side does not help; what helps is fewer interpreter copies per kernel (one `vm_run` call site; a large restructuring of `join_walk.inc`) or fewer instructions in each copy (the tiers; prewarm and the persisted pipeline cache pay the cost once per kernel change). Not adopted. What was done instead (2026-11-07): the aggregate-slot variants 4 / 8 / 12 became 4 / 12 (`scan_agg_variant()` in `scan_exec.h`; the 8-slot kernels are no longer built, the tables point index 1 at the 12-slot kernel), since the compile cost does not depend on the slot count: 208 instead of 259 SPIR-V files, `pgstrom-gpuserv` 492 -> 441 MB, half the kernels to prewarm. A query with 5-8 aggregates now runs the 12-slot interpreter kernel for its first task (the specialized kernel uses the same layout).
- **Where the scan test's extra time went (measured 2026-11-07).** Of the 1,070 s a full scan run took longer than on 6 Nov, three checks account for 905 s, each one cold pipeline creation of a 32-register Arrow/heap aggregate-over-join kernel for a geometry program (`ajagg32_4`: 301 s measured on its own; the 16-register kernels were warm). Two causes: the scan test gave the service its own per-cluster cache directory (`$ROOT/speccache`, deleted after a pass), so neither prewarm (which writes to `~/.cache/pgstrom`) nor earlier runs could help, and prewarm covered only the 16-register kernels. Now: `PGSTROM_GPUSERV_PIPELINE_CACHE_DIR` (the dev and scan scripts point it at the shared directory), a separate file for the specialized kernels (their number made the one file hit its cap and stop saving), caps of 1536 / 512 MB, the 32-register aggregation-over-join kernels in the prewarm list, `PREWARM=force`. `recheck_dev.sh` 345 s -> 146 s on the second run (`rk_j` 290 -> 18 s); the first run after a kernel change still pays the compile once. **Correction found afterwards (2026-11-07):** prewarm did not help the 32-register kernels because it only created the pipelines: the driver completes and records its compile at the first dispatch, so a pipeline that was never dispatched is compiled again by the next process (`ajagg32_4` 262-301 s in four processes in a row; `pcache_check`, which dispatches once, got 18 ms the second time; a never-compiled kernel through the fixed prewarm: 294.8 s, then 0.02 s). `do_prewarm` now finishes each pipeline with an empty dispatch (`gpuserv_main.c`). A forced prewarm of the whole list then took 1,230 s once (the four 32-register kernels at 240-266 s each, a few nogeom ones at 13-57 s); everything else was already warm.
- **Pitfalls hit:** the service Makefile did not track headers (stale objects: garbage self-test message, crashes inside MoltenVK) - now `-MMD`; an array enlarged without its `_ready` twin (`pipe_ready[3]`) corrupted the next member; the fuzzer's check mode compares error rows that are racy by nature (do not run `fuzz` through `verify_all.sh`).
- **Resource limits.** `locked_test.sh` builds with 3 jobs and runs tests at `nice -n 10`; test clusters use `pgconf_test.sh` (fsync off, autovacuum off, parallel workers capped). Same wall time as before (installcheck 132 s + scan 1541 s); `taskpolicy -b` made a dev script 5x slower and is opt-in.
- **Open:** one unkillable `close()` on the service socket (macOS kernel, ended when the service was killed) seen once in the `gpu_cache` installcheck test; one scan run in which the cluster went into crash recovery during the GiST index-split test (not reproduced).

## Smaller items (2026-11-05)

- **EvalPlanQual crash (found by `session_churn.sh`).** Concurrent `UPDATE`s over a GpuScan made the backend segfault in
  `heap_form_minimal_tuple`: `execCpuFallbackBaseTuple` (fallback.c) copied the columns of the CPU-fallback load list with
  `attno - 1`, but an UPDATE's scan carries the system column `ctid` (negative attno), so it read outside the slot arrays. It now
  fetches system columns with `heap_getsysattr`. This was very likely the cause of the unexplained crash recovery seen once in the
  GiST index-split test.
- **close() hang.** `xpuClientCloseSession` closed the socket while the session worker sat in `poll()` on it; macOS blocks there.
  Now `shutdown()`, join the worker, then `close()`.
- **verify_all.sh** copies the cluster log and any new `postgres-*.ips` / `pgstrom-gpuserv-*.ips` crash report of a failed stage into
  the stage's log directory.
- **GPU throttle: not needed.** Trace of a whole dev script: 142 GPU tasks, 1.26 s of GPU time in ~100 s (median 6 ms, max 37 ms), i.e.
  ~1% busy. The load that slows the machine is CPU (queries, page copies) and Metal's shader compiler, which runs as root (cannot be
  reniced); the lite kernels shrink the latter.
- **New on the GPU:** `&&&`, `ST_3DIntersects(point, point)`, `ST_HasZ/HasM/NDims/Zmflag/CoordDim` (probes `postgis/threed_probe*.sql/.out`).
  `ST_3DDistance` stays on the CPU: PostGIS' result differs from plain binary64 in 10,891 of 100,000 random pairs.

## Exact distances and lengths (2026-11-05, later)

The earlier A11 decision "no bit-exact emulation of PostGIS' distance" is reversed: the arithmetic is deterministic and known.
- **Probe** (`postgis/dist_probe.{sql,c,out}`, `len_probe.{sql,c,out}`): the installed PostGIS (3.7, arm64, contracting compiler) computes the
  distance of two points as `sqrt(fma(dz, dz, fma(dx, dx, dy*dy)))` (3D; the 2D form without dz) and the length of a line as the sum, left
  to right, of the same per-segment values: 0 differences in 200,000 point pairs and 60,000 lines (plain binary64: 11-22% differ).
  `ST_3DMaxDistance` of two points is the same value; an empty point gives NULL; a SRID mismatch raises; overflow/NaN give NULL or Infinity.
- **Soft fma:** `f64_sqr_add(a, c)` = RNE(a*a + c), c >= 0 (no cancellation, 128-bit frame), added to `f64_soft.inc`, checked bit for bit
  against the hardware `fma()` on 4,000,000 cases on the host and on the device (`f64soft_check`, op "sqr_add", index 31).
- **Ops:** `SOP_GEOMDIST` (138: ST_Distance, ST_3DDistance, ST_3DMaxDistance of two points, operands as ST_3DDWithin) and `SOP_GEOMLEN` (139:
  ST_Length, ST_Length2D, ST_3DLength of a LINESTRING; 0 for a point or polygon; multi-lines/collections/curves go back to the CPU).
- **Portability guard:** `vulkan_geom_dist_calibrated` / `vulkan_geom_len_calibrated` (codegen.c) call the installed PostGIS function on fixed
  point pairs / lines when the function is first planned and compare the bits with the host formula (`fma()` from libm); one difference
  and the function stays on the CPU for that backend (LOG line). A PostGIS built without contraction therefore keeps today's behaviour.
- **Possible next step:** ST_DWithin / ST_3DDWithin could use the exact distance instead of the 2^-47 band (no boundary rechecks).

## Distances, lengths and DWithin, exact (2026-11-05, evening)

- **ST_DWithin / ST_3DDWithin of two points** decide "exact distance <= tolerance" (`postgis/dwithin_probe.c`: 0 mismatches over 40,000 pairs at d and its float
  neighbours). The planner calls the installed PostGIS at d, nextafter(d, 0) and nextafter(d, inf) first (`vulkan_geom_dwithin_exact_calibrated`) and sets
  `KEXP_FLAG__GEOM_EXACT` on the expression; without it the kernel keeps the 2^-47 band (safe on any PostGIS build). The flag travels in `kexp->expflags`
  and `SOP_GDW_EXACT` (bit 30 of k1).
- **Lengths and perimeters** of multi-geometries and collections: `ST_Perimeter`, `ST_Perimeter2D`, `ST_3DPerimeter` and multi-parts for `ST_Length*`
  (`GEOMLEN` kinds 2-3). Each member's own total is added to its container's running total, a polygon's rings in order; 30,000 random geometries, 0 differences.
- **Distance of a point to a line, polygon, multi-geometry or collection** (2D, inside `GEOMDIST`): the minimum over the members of the distance to the first
  vertex and to each segment; a point in a polygon's exterior ring but in no hole is at 0, one in a hole at the distance to that hole's ring, one outside at the
  distance to the exterior ring. **The interior of a segment is PostGIS' `distance2d_pt_seg`, not the foot of the perpendicular**: `|s| * sqrt(L2)` with
  `s = fma(Ay-py, Bx-Ax, -((Ax-px)(By-Ay))) / L2`, `L2 = fma(Bx-Ax, Bx-Ax, (By-Ay)^2)` (`postgis/seg_probe.c`: 0 differences in 89,523 interior rows; the foot model
  failed the planner's calibration and the offline probe, but passed the first dev tests). A point whose projection `r` is within 2^-40 of 0 or 1 is re-checked
  (the fusing of `r` itself is not known to the last bit); a point near a polygon edge is re-checked by the winding test.
- **Software fma:** `f64_fma(a, b, c)` for any signs (the general case; `f64_sqr_add` is the cheap one for squares), exact against the hardware on 40 million host and
  16.7 million device cases including constructed cancellation.
- **Testing lesson:** a float8 in a projection can be computed by the CPU above the scan, so value tests compare in a **qual** against a value PostGIS stored when
  the table was built (`... where ST_Distance(g, pt) = dist`): any last-bit difference loses rows. Do this for every value-returning function.
- **Kernel size lesson:** the Slang compiler inlines every call; a heavy soft-float step written once but called from many places multiplies the kernel (the first
  point-to-geometry walker made every full scan kernel 28 MB instead of 5). Write walkers as single loops with one copy of each heavy step. The embedded SPIR-V is
  also no longer turned into C source (`gen_kspv.sh` uses `.incbin`): the C array had passed Clang's 2 GB source-location limit. Full kernels carry all geometry;
  a "no geometry" tier would shrink the 406 MB service binary (open).

**A14: rechecks in the aggregating join kernels (2026-11-06).** In `jagg` / `jgagg` (`JW_MODE` 1-3) an outer row whose scan quals returned a recheck (`g_recheck`: float4 denormal / underflow, numeric division by zero or beyond 64 bits, ...) was `hit = false` and silently omitted (measured: a float4 qual over a table with denormals gave 376732 rows against 377074; a recheck in the projection, hash key or join qual stopped the query with a kernel error). Two mechanisms, chosen per program by `scan_prog_build_join` from the opcodes (`op_may_recheck`: numeric, jsonb, geometry groups, `SOP_FARITH`, string compare / like / length): **`rck_outer`** (a recheck-capable instruction in the scan quals) and **`rck_walk`** (one after them: hash key, join qual, projection, aggregate arguments, FILTER; always for a GiST join). (1) Qual-only: the row has contributed nothing, so the kernel sets a bit (line pointers before the page + lp; Arrow: row of the pass) in a bitmap behind the results (`outb`, program header word 19 = its word offset, patched into the program copy, 0 = off) and the service hands the flagged rows to the backend (`joinagg_harvest_flags`, `arrow_exec.c`), no extra pass. (2) Walk / projection: an accumulator cannot retract, so the GiST scout pass is now generic: the VM join kernel (mode 0, `kparam.extra = 1`, poison records only) runs first, its poisoned rows go to the backend as `KDS_FORMAT_RECHECK` and into the skip bitmap (header word 18), which the aggregating pass honours (also in the specialized kernels and the lite variants: the `VM_NO_GEOM` guards are gone). The scout's record buffer starts at 4096 records and is rerun once with the exact count. Hash-key recheck is now a poison record in mode 0 (it was a kernel error). Programs without a recheck-capable instruction run no scout and write no flag: kernels cost only two header-word loads per outer row. The backend path is the one of the GiST case (`execCpuRecheckOneChunk` -> `execCpuFallbackBaseTuple`, hash depths through the hash fallback); RIGHT/FULL rows are flagged by the CPU join as for any rechecked row. Text and numeric *columns* are NULL-only in join programs, so a compressed or out-of-line outer string cannot recheck inside a join kernel (a text qual runs in a GpuScan below the join, which has its own recheck path); the tests still carry such strings. Tests: `joinagg_tests.inc` section "rows the device cannot decide" (float4 denormal qual ungrouped / grouped by inner and outer key, float4 in the projection and in a FILTER (scout), text qual over toasted strings, LEFT / RIGHT / FULL, numeric division by zero and overflow in a LEFT ON qual) and `arrow_tests.inc` (float4 underflow, qual and projection, LEFT). Timings: see the report of the commit; a scout pass roughly doubles the kernel time of the programs that need it.

## A14 status (coverage assertion green with 265 blocked shapes measured, runtime CPU fallback implemented: every JOIN and GROUP BY on the GPU, the CPU only as the fallback)

**What the milestone asserts.** For the join and GROUP BY shapes PostgreSQL can plan, the GPU runs them whenever the planner offers a GPU path, and the CPU runs them only when (a) the shape is on the allowlist with a reason, or (b) the GPU path failed at run time and the query was re-executed on the CPU with the same result. Both halves are tested:

1. **Coverage assertion** (`test/gpuserv/coverage_test.sh`, written: see the result below). A corpus of shapes (every join type with one to four inner relations; hash and nest-loop; keys of each type; GROUP BY with one, two and three keys of each type; each aggregate function; many and few groups; each source: heap, Arrow, Parquet, GPU-Cache) is run through `EXPLAIN` with the GPU enabled and the cost knobs that make the GPU path win. Each shape must contain `GpuJoin` / `GpuPreAgg`, unless `test/gpuserv/coverage_cpu_allowed.txt` lists it as `shape | reason | kind` with kind `by-design` (user-defined functions, composite types, ordered-set aggregates, `DISTINCT` aggregates, ...) or `gap` (a milestone below). A test fails when a shape that is not listed stays on the CPU, and also when a listed shape does reach the GPU (so the list can only shrink). The rows of every shape equal the CPU oracle's.
2. **Fallback assertion.** Today a program the service refuses (too many registers, an opcode it lacks, a device lost mid-query) fails the query: "no CPU fallback for a refused program" (known failure 15-16, fuzzer notes). The milestone makes the backend re-run the task, the node or the query on the CPU. Fault injection (an env/GUC that makes the service refuse or drop a program, kill the service mid-query, mark the device lost) must return the CPU result, not an error.

**Coverage assertion: written and green (2026-11-08).** `test/gpuserv/coverage_test.sh` (script + `coverage_shapes.py`, the corpus generator, + `coverage_cpu_allowed.txt`, the allowlist) runs 614 shapes: every join type with one to five inner relations (hash and forced nest-loop), join keys of 13 types x inner/LEFT/semi/anti (+ two-column, three-column, expression keys), aggregation over each join type, GROUP BY with one, two and three keys of each type, 90+ aggregate shapes (every numeric type x count/sum/avg/min/max, grouped and not; bool, text, DISTINCT, FILTER, ordered-set, string/array/jsonb_agg, bit, variance family), group counts 5 / 500 / 50,000 / 1,000,000 (default limit and raised), the same table as heap, Arrow, Parquet and GPU-Cache (the Arrow and Parquet files are written from the heap table by `pg2arrow`; uuid and unconstrained numeric cannot be written by it), and PostGIS GiST joins/aggregates. Each shape is planned with the GPU enabled and the cost knobs of the other dev scripts; a shape on the GPU is executed and its rows (md5 of the sorted rows) must equal `pg_strom.enabled=off`. First result: **320 of 614 shapes plan on the GPU, 294 stay on the CPU (29 by-design, 265 gap), 0 row differences.** The allowlist matches shape ids with globs and gives each a reason and a kind; the test fails for an unlisted CPU shape, for a listed shape that reaches the GPU, and for a glob that matches nothing, so the list can only shrink (`COVERAGE_ONLY=<regex>` and `COVERAGE_ROWS=0` for development; the planner's own DEBUG2 reason of every CPU shape is kept in `work/cpu_shapes.tsv`). Run time warm: see the commit message; the specialized kernels are switched off for it (`PGSTROM_GPUSERV_SPECIALIZE=off`; `spec_dev.sh` covers them).

**Runtime CPU fallback (first work item; implemented on branch `a14-fallback`, status below).** A node whose GPU session is refused or dies continues on the CPU and returns the rows of the plan with `pg_strom.enabled = off` (GUC `pg_strom.gpu_error_fallback`, default on). Design (`src/fallback.c`, `src/executor.c`):

- *No second plan is needed.* The CPU evaluator that already re-checks the rows the GPU cannot decide (`execCpuFallbackBaseTuple`: base quals, hash/nest-loop/GiST joins against the host copy of the inner buffers, the projection or the per-row partial-aggregate state) is the engine; "CPU mode" reads the node's own relation and feeds every visible row to it. Host quals, projection and the Agg above apply as usual.
- *The journal.* Every task sent to the service is recorded (`pgstromJournalAppend`): its heap blocks (`KDS_BLOCK_BLCKNR`), Arrow record batch / Parquet row group, or "the whole table" for a GPU-Cache scan. Replies of one connection arrive in command order (`handle_task_exec` is synchronous), so a consumed reply acknowledges the oldest entry (`pgstromJournalAck`). On a failure (an error reply of any kind, `terminated`, a failed write, the service not ready, connect failing) `pgstromCpuModeBegin` closes the session and the CPU repeats the unacknowledged entries, then claims the rest of the scan from the same shared counters the GPU path uses (`scan_block_count`, BRIN chunks, the Arrow record-batch counter, the GPU-Cache single-task counter), so parallel workers neither skip nor repeat blocks. Heap blocks are read with the snapshot's visibility rules (copy the tuples, release the buffer lock, then evaluate); an Arrow batch is decoded row by row with the recheck decoder.
- *Why every error falls back, data errors included.* The CPU raises the same error if PostgreSQL would, and does not raise one the GPU raised only because it evaluates quals in another order. The cost is one repeated scan for a query that fails anyway.
- *GpuPreAgg* keeps its partial aggregates inside the session, so all its tasks are repeated, and the recheck/fallback tuples a lost session would have returned are held back until Final (`defer_fallback`) so that nothing is emitted twice.
- *RIGHT/FULL joins*: the CPU sets the same (idempotent) outer-join map flags; the last worker emits the unmatched inner rows as with a working session (`final_is_last`).
- *Not recoverable, the original error is raised* (documented in `doc/parameters.md`): GPU-Sort (the parent Sort node was removed), pinned inner buffers and SELECT INTO direct (rows never reach the host), a partition-wise scan whose scan relations have different column counts, and `pg_strom.gpu_error_fallback = off`.
- *Fault injection.* `pg_strom.gpuserv_fault` (superuser GUC, carried in the session; `open`, `refuse[=N]`, `lost[=N]`, `drop[=N]`, `exit[=N]`, `final`, `node=`, `src=`) makes the service fail one query deterministically; `pg_strom.scan_chunk_blocks` makes heap scans send many small tasks; `pg_strom.debug_no_program_limits` switches the planner's program-size gates off so that the service really refuses. Tests: `test/gpuserv/fallback_tests.inc`, run by `fallback_dev.sh` and by `scan_test.sh`; `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows `cpu-mode: N worker(s), M task(s)` in `Fallback-stats`.
- *Covered by the tests (104 checks, all equal to the `pg_strom.enabled = off` rows):* GpuScan, GpuJoin (inner, left, right, full, semi, anti, two inner relations) and GpuPreAgg (with and without GROUP BY, over inner and right joins) on heap tables, Arrow and Parquet; GpuScan and GpuPreAgg over a GPU-Cache; faults `open`, `refuse` at task 1/2/3, `lost`, `drop` (a closed connection) at several tasks, `final`; parallel workers (scan, aggregation, join); a scan inside a transaction with its own inserts, updates and deletes; a real service restart (`exit=2`, the launcher restarts it and the next query runs on the GPU again); two real refusals (40 referenced columns: "too many kernel variables"; two 40-column tables joined: "unsupported join session"; 16 aggregates: "17 output columns (up to 12 are supported)").
- *Documented as errors:* GPU-Sort, pinned buffers, SELECT INTO direct, `pg_strom.gpu_error_fallback = off`; once a node is in CPU mode it does not try the service again (until a rescan).
- *Open:* item 15 of `vulkan_known_failures.txt` (pglz error in a recheck/sorted path) is a backend bug and is not masked by the fallback; item 16 (fuzzer seed 9) was not re-run; `test/sql/gpu_cache.sql` still runs its 35-column join with `enable_gpujoin = off` (the join would now fall back instead; the expected file was not regenerated); the CPU run of a node is single-threaded per process and reads the relation through the shared buffers (a long GpuPreAgg replay is as slow as the CPU plan, plus the repeated work); `pg_strom.gpu_error_fallback` repeats a query whose data error the CPU raises as well (cost: the first GPU attempt).


**Measured gaps** (shapes blocked, ordered; the milestone is where the work belongs; "key type" = `scan_vm_groupkey_type_supported()`: integers and the date/time types only):

(Heap-table GROUP BY with many groups is done: see "A3, many groups".)

| Shapes | Gap | Cause found | Work item |
|---|---|---|---|
| 31 | Hash-join key text / varchar / bpchar (also under aggregation, over Arrow/Parquet/GPU-Cache) | `gpu_join.c` offers a GpuJoin only when the key has a device type in the key list; `texteq` / `bpchareq` are not device hash-join functions | A6 text keys |
| 31 | Float aggregates (**closed 2026-11-08**, branch a10-float) | `sum(float4)`; `avg`/`min`/`max`/`sum` of float4/float8 with GROUP BY | A10 |
| 25 | GROUP BY key text / varchar / bpchar | not in the key list | A6 |
| 21 | GROUP BY with three or more keys | the kernels hold at most two keys (one, two and a numeric key work) | A3 |
| 18 | numeric aggregates | `min`/`max(numeric)`; `sum`/`avg` of a numeric without a declared scale (a `numeric(p,s)` column works, also grouped) | A6 numeric |
| 16 | Hash-join key float4 / float8 | key list | A6 |
| 14 | GROUP BY key bool | rejected as "unsupported device type (boolean)", although the key list names bool | A6 |
| 13 | GROUP BY key float4 / float8 | key list | A6 |
| 11 | `min`/`max` of text / varchar / bpchar | not device aggregates | A6 |
| 10 | Nest-loop joins (non-equi, range, cross, LATERAL, `&&` without an index) | no GpuNestLoop: `gpu_join.c` builds hash and GiST joins only (an equi join with `enable_hashjoin=off` still gets its GpuJoin) | A4 |
| 9 | Hash-join key numeric | `numeric_eq` has no device hash-join function (numeric GROUP BY keys do work) | A6 numeric |
| 8 | Hash-join key bool | `booleq` "not supported on the target device" | A6 |
| 8 | Decimal columns of Arrow / Parquet files | the scan stays a plain Foreign Scan, no GpuScan path at all (an `af` table with a numeric column) | A5 leftover |
| 8 (closed for float4/float8 on 2026-11-09 and for int2/int4/int8 on 2026-11-10, except `regr_count`; numeric stays on the CPU) | variance / stddev / corr / regr / covar | no device transition functions | A10 part 5 |
| 7 | `bool_and` / `bool_or` / `every` | not device aggregates | A6 |
| 6 | Hash-join key uuid; 5: GROUP BY key uuid | `uuid_eq`; key list | A6 |
| 4 | GROUP BY a computed non-device key (`extract()`, `upper()`, CASE returning text, interval) | key type of the expression | A6 |
| 4 | Spatial joins outside the GiST forms (geometry outer operand, `ST_Intersects` / `ST_Distance` between two columns, `geometry = geometry`) | codegen has no device function for them | A13 |
| 3 | GROUPING SETS / ROLLUP / CUBE | GpuPreAgg does not take grouping sets | A14 |
| 3 | `bit_and` / `bit_or` / `bit_xor` | not device aggregates | A6 |
| 3 | Estimated groups above `pg_strom.gpupreagg_max_groups` (500, 50,000, 1,000,000 with the default limit; with the limit raised all three plan on the GPU and return the CPU's rows) | the planner's guard (the GPU is slower there) | A3 global-memory hash |
| 2 | GROUP BY a geometry column, or `st_x(geom)::numeric` | no device key | A13 |
| 1 each | computed jsonb text key (A8); `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` join clause; `UNION ALL` below the aggregate (Append); a CASE expression as an aggregate argument; GpuPreAgg over a GiST *semi* join (the path is built and loses on cost) | | A14, A14, A14, A6, A13 |
| 29 | By design | DISTINCT (9) and ordered-set (5) aggregates, aggregates with ORDER BY (4), `string_agg` / `array_agg` (5), `st_extent` / `st_union` (2), row and array GROUP BY keys (2), hashed `NOT IN` SubPlan, text order / LIKE under a non-C collation | |

What the corpus shows already works (all with the CPU's rows): inner / LEFT / RIGHT / FULL / semi / anti hash joins with one to four inner relations, and five inner relations as a chain of stacked GpuJoins (so "more than four inner relations stay on the CPU" in the older text is wrong); integer, date, time and timestamp join keys and GROUP BY keys (also numeric GROUP BY keys); multi-column integer join keys; a user-defined function in a scan qual below a GpuJoin; every numeric type x count/sum/avg/min/max, ungrouped (except `sum(float4)` and numeric `min`/`max`) and grouped (integers and `numeric(p,s)`); FILTER; GpuPreAgg over every join type; aggregation over a join of Arrow, Parquet and GPU-Cache sources; partitioned tables and views; the GiST joins (`&&`, `ST_DWithin`, `ST_Contains`, `ST_Within`, `ST_Intersects` against `ST_MakePoint(x, y)`, inner / LEFT / RIGHT / semi / anti, `GROUP BY` over them, a second hash join below). Corrections made to the docs after this run: `doc/features.md` listed GiST joins as "not yet" next to the GiST section, listed `bool` as a join key (it is not) and "more than four inner relations" as a limit; the README "Not yet" row now names the key and aggregate types.

**Order proposed:** the runtime fallback first (it makes every later gap safe to open), then A6 text/numeric keys and numeric in joins, A10, A3 many-groups GROUP BY, nest-loop joins, the rest. The allowlist holds one line per shape family (glob) with the measured reason, so the coverage test passes today and each closed gap deletes lines.

**Not in scope.** Operations that are not a join or a GROUP BY (window functions beyond the rank filter, sorts of scan results, DISTINCT outside aggregates) stay on their own milestones (A7). "Every shape" means every shape of the corpus; a user-defined function or a type outside the device's type list stays on the CPU by design and is listed as such.

**A14 slice 1a: text / varchar / bpchar hash-join keys (a6-keys, done 2026-11-08; 31 shapes closed: coverage 614 shapes, 351 on the GPU).** The join kernels hash the payload bytes of a string key with PostgreSQL's `hash_bytes` (`kernels_vm/hash_bytes.inc`, `SOP_HASHMERGE` with k1 = 1 text / 2 bpchar without trailing blanks; `test/gpuserv/hash_check.sh` compares it on the device with `hashtext()` for 0..40 bytes at four alignments: 164 cases) and compare them bytewise in the join qual (`texteq` / `bpchareq`: EQ and NE only are allowed in a join program). "C collation only" applies to ORDERING; equality and hashing are collation independent for every deterministic collation, so the planner (`gpu_join.c`) checks `get_collation_isdeterministic(inputcollid)` and a nondeterministic ICU collation stays on the CPU. The backend's text/bytea/bpchar hash functions now detoast first. Join programs got the constant pool (header word 20) and may load outer and inner string columns; an unreadable (compressed/out-of-line) key sets `rck_outer` (non-Arrow sources) / `rck_walk` (inner), so the backend joins that outer row. Cost measured (`keys_dev.sh`, 60,000 x 4,400 rows, best of 3 incl. psql): GPU aggregation over a join on a text key 334 ms against 241 ms on an integer key (the scout pass of `rck_walk` plus different match counts), a plain join count on the text key 141 ms. SPIR-V: `gpujoin8` 9,640,044 -> 9,784,612 bytes (+144,568, +1.5%), `ajgagg16_4` 9,648,324 -> 9,792,892; the cold Metal compile of both stayed at about 303 s (lite 4 s, nogeom 22 s). Tests: `keys_dev.sh` (heap, Arrow, Parquet, GPU-Cache; inner/LEFT/RIGHT/FULL/semi/anti; lengths 0..30, UTF-8, NULLs, bpchar(14) against bpchar(20), compressible 5 kB and STORAGE EXTERNAL keys): all passed.

**A6b: min / max of text, varchar, bpchar and uuid (slice 1 done 2026-11-11: min / max of text, varchar and bpchar WITHOUT GROUP BY on the GPU; GROUP BY, joins and uuid open; 11 coverage shapes at the start: `agg.fn.min_text*`, `max_text*`, `max_varchar`, `min_char`, `min_uuid`, `srcfull.*.fn.min_text`, `aggjoin.type.min_max_text`).** Split out of the a6-keys branch because it needs exact per-group extremes of variable-size values in several kernel families, each a cold compile. What A6 left in place for it: the string register `{offset | length << 32 | source << 60}` and `str_memcmp` / `str_truelen` in the VM, the per-task string-key table of A6 slice 1b (the table points into the task's pages and is flushed after every task: an extreme can be a pointer too), and the host dictionary of distinct strings. Design to start from: (1) planner: only the C collation (ordering is locale based otherwise; uuid is bytewise), a plain column of a heap, GPU-Cache or Arrow scan (no join at first); (2) a partial state that carries the value: a bytea holding the text varlena (empty = no value), `pgstrom.pmin(text)` / `pmax(text)`, `fmin_trans_text`, `fminmax_final_text`, aggregates `min_text` / `max_text` (also bpchar, which compares without trailing blanks, and uuid) in the unreleased 6.2--7.0 script, as the numeric min/max of A6 did; (3) service: per group the extreme is the pointer to the winning row's bytes, kept as one 32-bit word per slot (payload offset, source bit, a header-length bit: the length comes from the varlena header; Arrow and GPU-Cache strings have no header, so they need a second word or a per-pass resolve) updated by a compare-and-swap loop that compares the candidate with the current extreme byte by byte (lock-free: a failed swap means another thread improved it), and resolved to bytes on the host when the task's table is flushed (the pages are still there), merged by byte comparison into the session's groups; the ungrouped aggregation (agg.slang) keeps one pointer per thread and reduces them the same way; (4) the join aggregation kernels (`jagg`, `jgagg`) are at the Metal size limit: text min/max over a join stays on the CPU until R15-style tiers make room. Kernel edits once (gtab, agtab, agg, aagg and their lite/nogeom tiers), measure before and after.

**A6b status (2026-11-11, branch a6b, not pushed).** *Done, slice 1: ungrouped `min` / `max` of text, varchar and bpchar on heap, GPU-Cache, Arrow and Parquet scans.* (1) Planner (`gpu_preagg.c`): the catalog entries `min/max(text)` (varchar comes through `min(text)` and a RelabelType) and `min/max(bpchar)` carry the new actions `KAGG_ACTION__PMIN_TEXT` / `PMAX_TEXT` (305 / 405) and final code `KAGG_FINAL__FMINMAX_TEXT`; they are offered only when the aggregate's input collation is C (`__collate_is_c`, deterministic), the argument is a plain column (RelabelType / CollateExpr stripped, must be a Var) of a scan without a join, and the query has no GROUP BY (`scan_vm_agg_action_supported()` accepts the actions; a gate at the limits check refuses them with `groupClause`). (2) Partial state (`aggfuncs.c`, 6.2--7.0 script): `pgstrom.pmin/pmax(text | bpchar [, bool])` return the value as its varlena (header included) in a bytea, an empty bytea is "no value" (so `''` is a value), `fmin/fmax_trans_text|bpchar` merge with `bttextcmp` / `bpcharcmp` under C_COLLATION_OID, `fminmax_final_text|bpchar` rebuild a 4-byte-header varlena, aggregates `min_text`, `max_text`, `min_bpchar`, `max_bpchar` (over bytea); the filtered `pmin(numeric, bool)` no longer crashes on a NULL argument (found on the way). No uuid aggregate exists in PostgreSQL 17 (`min(gu::text)` is a computed string and stays on the CPU, by design). (3) Kernel (`agg.slang` only, so one cold compile: classes 14 text min, 15 text max, 16 / 17 the bpchar forms, which compare without trailing blanks): the per-thread accumulator is the 64-bit string register `{offset | length << 32 | source << 60}` of the winning row (compared with `str_memcmp` / `str_truelen`), the workgroup reduction compares registers the same way, and the record carries the register in the A words. The specializer refuses classes above 9 (`gen_fail`) so these programs stay on the interpreter; classes 14-17 do not force the nogeom tier (`scan_prog.c`). (4) Service: `agg_merge_workgroups()` resolves each workgroup's register to bytes in the page sources it still holds (`ps.baseF` / `baseL` for heap, `at.bg.map` for Arrow / Parquet / GPU-Cache) and keeps the best as a varlena in `agg_state.sval[]`; the final reply writes it as the bytea state (`agg_final_build`). A program with text min / max and GROUP BY, or in a join, is refused by `scan_prog.c` (the planner does not offer it; A14 would fall back). *Tests:* `minmaxtext_dev.sh` / `minmaxtext_tests.inc` (87 checks: the SQL functions against `min` / `max` including state merges with empty and NULL states, FILTER, one row and no rows, `''`, multi-byte, compressed and out-of-line values, bpchar blanks; the GPU path against `pg_strom.enabled=off` on heap, Arrow, Parquet and GPU-Cache with the plan asserted to be GpuPreAgg; grouped shapes asserted to stay on the CPU; the non-C collation, expression and join gates), also run with 600,000 rows and `PGSTROM_GPUSERV_SPECIALIZE=check`. Coverage: the shapes use `collate "C"` (the test cluster's default collation is not C); `agg.fn.min_text.ungrouped`, `max_text.ungrouped` and the new `srcfull.*.fn.min_text.ungrouped` reach the GPU; the grouped shapes and `aggjoin.type.min_max_text` stay listed (gap:A6b). *Cold compile:* `gpuagg8_4` 537 s and `aagg16_4` 563 s in the full tier (Metal), 1.7 s / 1.8 s lite and 8.7 s / 9.2 s nogeom (`prewarm_kernels.sh`, once after the change); 4,000,000 rows of `tmo` (minmaxtext_dev.sh, M1 Pro): `min(t), max(t), min(w)` takes 225-234 ms warm on the GPU (329 ms first) against 552-585 ms on PostgreSQL (2.4x), the same as `min(n), max(n), min(pay)` on the GPU (261-297 ms; CPU 439 ms): the string extreme adds no measurable time to the page-bound scan. *Open (design carried over, not done):* (a) GROUP BY: the shared-memory tables (`gagg.slang`: 32-bit slots, atomics, rounds) and the session-wide table (`gtab.slang`) hold ints; a string extreme needs a per-slot pointer word with a compare-and-swap loop that compares bytes, and `gtab` is flushed after every task while the pages go away, so the extreme must be resolved to bytes on the host before the flush (per touched group) or kept as a global row id; until then GROUP BY stays on the CPU (planner gate, `scan_prog.c` refusal). (b) The join aggregation kernels (`jagg`, `jgagg`) are at the Metal size limit: text min / max over a join stays on the CPU until the R15-style tiers make room. (c) uuid has no aggregate in PostgreSQL 17; if one appears (or for `min(uuid_col::text)` as a plain-column rewrite) the register and the 16-byte compare are the same.

**A6b status, slice 2 (branch a6b-groupby, not pushed): min / max of text, varchar and bpchar WITH GROUP BY on the GPU** (heap, GPU-Cache, Arrow, Parquet; joins stay on the CPU, uuid has no aggregate). *Atomics:* M1 (base tier) has no 64-bit atomics (`atomic_i64_add` missing in devcaps; desktop tier devices report `shaderBufferInt64Atomics`), so nothing relies on them: the design uses 32-bit words only. *Shared-memory table (`gagg.slang`, `agagg`):* no compare-and-swap; the slot's owner thread folds the round's staged rows in thread order (as float8 does) and keeps the 64-bit string register in `sh_l0/sh_l1`; the record carries it in L0/L1 and `group_merge_blocks` resolves it to bytes from the page sources (`srcs`: `ps.baseF/baseL`, `at.bg.map`) and merges by byte comparison. `has_f` now covers classes 6-13 only, text has its own fold outside `VM_NO_F8` (so the lite tier has it). *Session table (`gtab.slang`, `agtab`):* a new mode 4 dispatch after the rows have their slots. Modes 0/2 write per row a side entry in `outb` behind the flags (`{slot word index, register + valid bit 62}` per text aggregate; program words 22/23 carry its base and width), mode 4 runs a bounded compare-and-swap loop on the slot's word (the winning row + 1) comparing the registers' bytes (the visibility rule: only data of earlier dispatches or the read-only pages is read). After mode 4 the host (`scan_gtab_text_collect`) resolves each slot's winner to bytes, interns it in the session's string dictionary (`str_dict`) and keeps the id per slot; `gtab_flush` merges it into the group (`agg_acc.val` = dictionary id), so the existing per-task flush of the string-key lifecycle is reused (`scan_gtab_per_task`: string keys or text extremes). Overflow rows (`group_cpu_rows`, the Arrow overflow lists, `acc_add_ov`) resolve their registers the same way (`acc_text_reg`), which also covers the CPU VM's rows. The final reply builds the bytea state per group from the dictionary. The specializer still refuses classes above 9, so these programs run on the VM kernels; the planner gate and the `scan_prog.c` refusal are gone. *Tests:* `minmaxtext_dev.sh` (grouped shapes now GpuPreAgg-asserted and equal to the CPU on tmo / tmoa / tmop / tmog: 12 groups, 3000 groups, text / varchar keys, FILTER, NULL / empty groups, bpchar; a section restarts the cluster with `PGSTROM_GPUSERV_GTAB=0` to force gagg overflow rows); the grouped `gap:A6b` entries left `coverage_cpu_allowed.txt` (the join one and uuid stay).


## A11/A13 remaining work (planned 2026-11-08: 2D and 3D PostGIS on the GPU, the CPU as the fallback)

What is on the GPU is listed in `doc/features.md` ("PostGIS `geometry`", "GiST-index spatial join"). Everything below is not, and was found by reading the feature rows and searching `scan_caps.c` / `codegen.c` for each function name (none of the functions in R3, R4, R5 appears there). **Rules for every slice** (standing): Z, M and ZM geometries and the 3D variants of a function are part of the same slice, not a later one ([[feedback-postgis-3d-with-2d]]); the result is PostGIS' own, bit for bit (`vulkan_geom_*_calibrated` guard in `codegen.c` for each function that returns a value, so that a PostGIS built differently keeps the function on the CPU); a row the kernel cannot decide goes to the backend through the recheck path (A12), never to a wrong answer; tests are differential against `pg_strom.enabled=off` on heap, Arrow/Parquet and GPU-Cache sources, with empty, NULL, non-finite, extreme-coordinate and mixed-SRID inputs; GPU first, CPU only as the fallback. The runtime CPU fallback for a refused program (A14) is a prerequisite for opening these wide.

| Id | Item | Notes / dependencies |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Inner NON-POINT `ST_DWithin` / `ST_3DDWithin` / distance comparisons in the GiST join (inner line, polygon, multi-geometry) | **Scan side done and merged (2026-11-08)**: exact 2D/3D point-to-line/polygon/multi-geometry in scan quals, aggregates, Arrow, Parquet and GPU-Cache (not faster than PostgreSQL in a scan: 500 ms against 70-250 ms, the scan floor dominates). **Join side blocked by Metal's compile limit, not by exactness**: the exact walker adds 0.8 MB to a join kernel that is already 9.64 MB (geometry is 8.8 MB of it), the full variant compiled for more than 900 s and failed twice with `XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED`; the join kernels were left byte-identical and inner lines/polygons stay poison records joined on the CPU (75,351 rows handed back took 34 s against 0.23 s in PostgreSQL: keep `pg_strom.enable_gpugistindex` off for non-point inner relations). **Join side done by R15 (2026-11-09)**: the walker is the join kernel tier `gwalk` (7.4 MB, 84 s cold on Metal), chosen for a program whose ST_DWithin carries `KEXP_FLAG__GEOM_NP` (a plain `geometry` inner column); lines, polygons and multi-geometries are decided in the join, only unreadable values (Z/M, empty, huge, non-finite) stay poison records |
| R15 | **Geometry tiers of the join kernels (done 2026-11-09)** | Measured first (join16, SPIR-V bytes, slangc 2026.20; base with no optional group 1.05 MB; each group alone on top of it): BOX +1.5 MB, PIP +2.1, point DWithin +1.3, 3DDWithin +1.8, point Distance +1.1, the three together +4.2, the walker (non-point operands, includes the three) +5.5 in all, ZM +0.1, FLAG +0.2, LEN (length, perimeter, area) +6.9; the optional numeric +0.23, float8 +0.68, jsonb +1.16; the groups add up. Scan kernels (scan16: 0.24 MB without geometry, 6.2 MB with all of it) were left alone. Cold Metal compile (one at a time, join16 with numeric and float8, no jsonb): point DWithin 3.2 MB 13 s; DWithin + 3DDWithin 5.0 MB 22 s; walker 7.4 MB 84 s; point in polygon 4.05 MB 150 s (the size does not predict it: PIP's ring loops cost Metal); the former full tier 9.8 MB 294-329 s. **Design (option a)**: `VM_GEOM_MASK` (`scan_common.slang`, groups `VM_G_*`) picks the geometry instructions a kernel interprets; `prog->feat` carries the groups a program uses (bits 4 up, `scan_insn_feat(op, k0)`: ST_DWithin with `SOP_GDW_NP` needs the walker group); `scan_join_tier()` picks, for the join families only (join, jagg, jgagg and their Arrow twins; registers 16/32), the smallest tier: `gpip` (PIP), `gdw` (point DWithin), `gwalk` (walker); anything else (several groups, jsonb with geometry, 3DDWithin of points alone) runs on the unchanged full tier, whose SPIR-V is byte-identical to before (checked with cmp for join, ajoin, jagg, scan, arrow, gagg, nogeom and lite kernels: nothing else recompiles). The planner clears `KEXP_FLAG__GEOM_NP` of a join ST_DWithin whose geometry columns are declared `geometry(Point...)` (typmod), so that a point-only join takes `gdw` instead of the walker; a plain `geometry` column takes `gwalk`. The point-to-point decisions of the walker/new tiers also check PostGIS' FLT_MAX initial distance (the full join tier still does not, as before). Cost: 60 more SPIR-V files (3 tiers x 20 kernels), `pgstrom-gpuserv` 441 -> 825 MB, 3 more minutes of build; the specializer defines the same `VM_GEOM_MASK` so check mode compares like with like. Not done: a `gdw3` tier (3DDWithin of points alone still uses the 9.8 MB full tier or, with a plain column, the walker), geometry tiers for scan families, BOX/ZM/FLAG/LEN in joins (the planner keeps them out of join programs). Tests: `gist_tests.inc` (R15 section), `feat_check`. |
| R2 | 3D distance point-to-line / polygon / multi-geometry (`ST_3DDistance`, `ST_3DDWithin` with a non-point operand) | After R1; same fma-exact segment arithmetic, a Z-aware projection |
| R3 | Measures: `ST_Area` (polygons with holes, multipolygons, collections), `ST_NPoints`, `ST_NumGeometries`, `ST_NumPoints`; Z is ignored by `ST_Area` as in PostGIS (`ST_3DArea` comes from the SFCGAL extension and is out of scope) | Same ring walk as the perimeter code | **Status (2026-11-08): done, device test pending its last run.** `ST_Area` is GEOMLEN kind 4: PostGIS 3.7 does NOT sum x*(y2-y1); `ptarray_signed_area` sums the fan-triangle determinants with Kahan compensation plus error tails and falls back to an exact expansion under a 1e-12 cancellation test, which `kernels_vm/area_ring.inc` reproduces in software binary64 (one text for the kernel, `cpu_vm.c` and the planner guard `vulkan_geom_area_calibrated` via `svc_area.c`); cancellation, rescaling (products > 2^990), non-finite values, curves/TIN/triangles go to the backend. `ST_NPoints`, `ST_NumPoints`, `ST_NumGeometries`, `ST_NRings`, `ST_NumInteriorRings`: GEOMFLAG kinds 6-10 (`vm_geom_cnt`). Probes: `postgis/measure_probe.*`, `area_probe*.sql`, `area_twin_check.c` (240,000 ring comparisons, 0 wrong). The aggregate-argument replay bug (`count(ST_Area(g))` with a qual lost the rechecked rows) was fixed in fallback.c by the A14 replay work; repro `postgis/area_agg_repro.sql`. Verified 2026-11-09: postgis3d_dev 281 ok, postgis_dev 584 ok, gpucache_dev 224 ok, spec_compile_check 20 ok, 0 failures. Cost: 100,000 polygons of 41 points, `count(*) where ST_Area(g) > 100` takes 2.47 s on the GPU (warm) against 1.71 s on 8-less PostgreSQL (one process); min/max 5.4 s against 3.7 s; the software binary64 area (about 150 soft-f64 operations per vertex) makes ST_Area slower than the CPU, so it is offered for correctness and for filters next to other GPU work, not for speed. `sum(ST_Area(g))` runs on the GPU (GpuPreAgg psum) but its float8 sum differs from PostgreSQL in the last digits (12045453.808097763 against 12045453.80809465: a different summation order, as for every float8 sum), min/max are identical.
| R4 | Accessors: `ST_X`, `ST_Y` (with `ST_Z`, `ST_M` done), `ST_SRID`, `ST_IsEmpty`, `ST_GeometryType`, `ST_StartPoint`/`ST_EndPoint` returning a point | Cheap; the point-returning ones need computed geometry values in projections, which the VM does not hold yet (they feed other functions only) | **Status (2026-11-08): done except as noted.** `ST_X`/`ST_Y` are GEOMZM kinds 2/3, `ST_SRID` GEOMFLAG kind 5, `ST_IsEmpty` kind 11 (calibration guard `vulkan_geom_acc_calibrated`). `ST_GeometryType`/`GeometryType` (computed text) and `ST_StartPoint`/`ST_EndPoint` (computed geometry) stay on the CPU: the VM builds neither. Heap and GPU-Cache only: Arrow_Fdw has no geometry column type.
| R5 | Geometry-against-geometry predicates: `ST_Intersects`, `ST_Contains`, `ST_Within`, `ST_Covers`, `ST_CoveredBy` for line/polygon operands (segment intersection, ring containment) | Biggest slice; exact orientation tests (software fma); 3D variants decide on the 2D shape as PostGIS does | | **Status (slice 1 done, 2026-11-10): scan quals (heap, GPU-Cache; aggregates over them), not join programs.** `kernels_vm/geom_pred.inc` (one text for the kernel and `cpu_vm.c`, inside `vm_geom_pip` under `#ifndef WITH_INNER`: the join kernels are byte-identical, `gist_dev` unchanged) decides ST_Intersects / Contains / Within / Covers / CoveredBy of two line / polygon / multi-line / multi-polygon operands, 2D, Z, M, ZM (X and Y only; the cached box of a Z/M geometry is skipped by its size). Not exact arithmetic: a pair is decided only beyond the orientation error bound (2^-49 of the term sum, as `pip_ring`), so GEOS' answer is the answer; touching / shared vertices / collinear overlap / vertex on an edge go to the backend (RECHECK). Intersects: boxes apart false; a certain proper crossing true; any unsure pair recheck; else the first vertex of each line / polygon shell inside the other's area (certain point-in-polygon) true, else false. Contains/Covers (same answers when nothing touches): a line container is rechecked unless the boxes say no; box not inside false; crossing false; a root of B outside A false; unsure recheck; a hole of A with a vertex inside area B false; else true. A ring whose winding number and crossing parity disagree (self-overlapping, GEOS uses parity) is rechecked; empty, open, non-finite, collection, multipoint, curve, geodetic operands, other SRID (PostGIS raises) and more than 16,384 segment pairs too. Booleans decided outside an error bound need no calibration guard. ST_3DIntersects of non-points is a 3D distance test and stays on the CPU. Tests: `test/gpuserv/postgis_pred_tests.inc` (`PRED_ONLY=1 ... postgis_dev.sh`; 2D/Z/M/ZM tables, constants of four shapes, pentagram rings, SRIDs, big pairs, recheck counters). **Slice 2 (R5 remainder + R6 + R8, 2026-11-11):** point / multipoint against line / multi-line / area for Intersects / Contains / Within / Covers / CoveredBy (`gp_pt`: a point certainly off every segment by the same orientation bound, or certainly in/out of an area; on a line or too close is RECHECK; Contains(points, extent) false); Z/M/ZM polygons against points (non-fused through `gp_pt`, fused `ST_MakePoint` through `gp_pip_area` with the polygon's own stride; 2D polygon + single point keeps the old path with the cached box); `ST_Disjoint` = negated Intersects (fused points allowed); `ST_Touches` (false when a crossing or no touch, else recheck), `ST_Crosses` (area/area false; line/area true on a certain crossing; line/line true on certain crossings only with no unsure pair anywhere), `ST_Overlaps` (dimension mismatch false; area/area true on a certain crossing; line/line recheck on any unsure pair else false), `ST_Equals` (envelope or dimension differ false; boundaries apart false; else recheck): the relation is `kind - 2` in bits 2-4 of the GEOMPIP `k0` (no new opcode, same geometry tier), scan quals only (`vulkan_text_func_allowed` refuses them in join programs). Probe: `test/gpuserv/postgis/rel_probe.sql`. The nested ternaries of slice 1 are if/else now (NVVM). **Left:** pair cap and soft-float cost per segment pair, the same in a GiST join (poison records; needs the compile probe), TRUE answers of Touches/Equals (need exact predicates), points in Crosses/Overlaps/Equals/Touches (point/line, multipoint) stay on the CPU. **Left (slices 3+):** the GiST join (poison records; needs a compile probe), Arrow/Parquet have no geometry column, the pair cap and the soft-float cost per segment pair (a spatial index of the segments would lift both).
| R6 | `ST_Disjoint`, `ST_Touches`, `ST_Crosses`, `ST_Overlaps`, `ST_Equals` | Need the DE-9IM relations of R5; decided on the device where the relation is clear, the rest rechecked |
| R7 | `ST_Relate` (pattern), `ST_Distance` between lines/polygons, `ST_ClosestPoint`, `ST_MaxDistance`, Hausdorff | After R5/R6; may stay on the CPU by design if the cost model says so |
| R8 | Z/M polygons and geodetic (`geography`-like SRIDs) polygons: today re-checked per row | Z/M polygons are 2D for the predicates, so the device can decide them; geodetic ones stay on the CPU |
| R9 | GiST join: RIGHT and FULL joins under `GpuPreAgg`, GPU-Sort of the result | Same obstacle as the non-spatial RIGHT/FULL aggregation (done for hash joins, "GpuPreAgg over RIGHT and FULL joins") plus the poison-record rejoin |
| R10 | GiST join: nearest-neighbour `<->` (KNN order by) and `ST_DWithin` with a column tolerance on both sides | Needs a priority-queue walk; may be a separate kernel |
| R11 | Indexes other than GiST (SP-GiST, BRIN on geometry) and spatial predicates in nest-loop joins | Nest-loop joins are an A4/A14 item |
| R12 | Constructors and processing that return geometries: `ST_Buffer`, `ST_Union`, `ST_Intersection`, `ST_Simplify`, `ST_Collect`, `ST_Envelope`, `ST_Centroid`, `ST_ConvexHull`, `ST_Transform` (needs PROJ), `ST_AsText` / `ST_AsGeoJSON` / `ST_GeomFromText` | Variable-length geometry output in the result path (the A8 limit for computed text/jsonb); `ST_Envelope` and `ST_Centroid` first, the rest decided case by case, PROJ-based ones on the CPU by design |
| R13 | Types: `geography`, curved geometries (CIRCULARSTRING, ...), TIN, raster | `geography` is geodetic arithmetic (R8); the others stay on the CPU by design unless a use case appears |
| R14 | Calibration and portability: the `vulkan_geom_*_calibrated` guards for every new function, a build of the tests against PostGIS with and without floating-point contraction, PG 15/16/18/19 expected files | Run once per slice; needs the other PostgreSQL versions installed |

**Suggested order:** R1 (running), R3 + R4 (small, widen what is offered), R5, R2, R6, R9, R7/R8, R10-R14. R5 is the one that changes how much of a typical spatial query lands on the GPU: most real queries test lines or polygons against polygons, not points.

**A14 execution order from the measured gap table (2026-11-08, 614 shapes, 320 on the GPU, 294 on the CPU: 29 by design, 265 gaps).** By shapes blocked: (1) text/varchar/bpchar as hash-join key (31) and GROUP BY key (25), `min`/`max` of text (11) -> A6 string keys; (2) float aggregates with GROUP BY and `sum(float4)` (31), `variance`/`stddev`/`corr`/`regr` (8) -> A10; float4/float8 join keys (16) and GROUP BY keys (13), bool keys (22), uuid keys (11), numeric join keys (9), numeric `min`/`max` and unscaled-numeric `sum`/`avg` (18), `bool_and`/`bool_or`/`every` (7) -> the rest of A6; (3) three or more GROUP BY keys (21) and the group-count limit (3) -> A3; (4) nest-loop joins (10) -> A4; (5) Decimal columns in Arrow/Parquet (8) -> A5; GROUPING SETS (3). The runtime CPU fallback (agent `a14-fallback`) and R15 (geometry out of the join kernel) run alongside. Each closed gap deletes its line from `test/gpuserv/coverage_cpu_allowed.txt`.


## A15 status (planned): optional SFCGAL on the GPU

Study of 2026-11-09 (no code, no GPU test). Facts about the installed stack were read locally: PostGIS 3.7.0 (`postgis_sfcgal--3.7.0.sql`), SFCGAL 2.3.0, CGAL 6.2.1 (Homebrew headers); algorithm details were read on the web and are marked [web]. Items marked (verify) are from memory of the sources and must be checked by the spike before anyone relies on them.

**1. Inventory (PostGIS 3.7.0: 59 `CG_*` functions, 18 `ST_*` names; `postgis_sfcgal_version/full_version/scripts_installed/noop` are management).** In 3.7 the `ST_*` names are SQL wrappers that raise a deprecation notice and call the `CG_*` C function (e.g. `ST_3DArea` calls `CG_3DArea`, Availability 3.5.0); `CG_Area`, `CG_Intersects`, `CG_3DIntersects`, `CG_Distance`, `CG_3DDistance`, `CG_Union`, `CG_Difference`, `CG_Intersection` have no `ST_` name. `ST_3DIntersects` and `ST_3DDistance` of core PostGIS are not SFCGAL (R2/R5 scope). The SFCGAL kernel is `CGAL::Exact_predicates_exact_constructions_kernel` (Epeck, lazy `Gmpq`; `SFCGAL/Kernel.h`).

| Class | Functions | Algorithm / exactness | GPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scalar measure | `CG_3DArea`/`ST_3DArea`, `CG_Area`, `CG_Volume`/`ST_Volume` | `area3D` [web, area.cpp]: per ring the cross products are summed in the exact kernel, then `to_double(squared_length)`, `sqrt` in double, `0.5*`, sums of doubles; `volume` returns an exact `Kernel::FT` (sum of signed tetrahedra) that PostGIS converts to double. Needs exact polynomial evaluation of degree 3-4 in the input doubles, then one rounding | yes, with exact expansions (feasible) |
| Scalar predicate | `CG_Orientation`, `CG_IsPlanar`, `CG_IsSolid` | Sign of an exact area (orientation), exact `coplanar` (orient3d) test, a type test | yes: orientation and planarity are exact signs (expansions, no rationals); `IsSolid` is a tag check, pointless on the GPU |
| Pair predicate | `CG_3DIntersects`, `CG_Intersects` | CGAL `do_intersect` on triangles/segments/points: exact predicates only, no constructions (verify) | culling only, see 3 |
| Pair measure | `CG_Distance`, `CG_3DDistance` | exact squared distance (a construction) then `sqrt(to_double)` | later, if the spike says so |
| Constructive, predicates only | `CG_3DConvexHull`, `CG_Tesselate`/`CG_Triangulate`, `CG_ConstrainedDelaunayTriangles`, `CG_AlphaShape`, `CG_OptimalAlphaShape`, partitions (`ApproxConvex`, `GreeneApprox`, `OptimalConvex`, `YMonotone`) | Output vertices are input points; sequential incremental algorithms with exact predicates | CPU by design |
| Constructive, exact constructions | `CG_3DUnion/Intersection/Difference` (+ 2D `CG_Union/Intersection/Difference`, aggregates `CG_3DUnion`, `CG_Union`), `CG_StraightSkeleton`, `CG_StraightSkeletonPartition`, `CG_ApproximateMedialAxis`, `CG_ExtrudeStraightSkeleton`, `CG_Generate*Roof`, `CG_MinkowskiSum`, `CG_Visibility`, `CG_3DBuffer`, `CG_3DAlphaWrapping`, `CG_PolygonRepair`, `CG_Simplify` | New vertices are rational (intersection points, skeleton events); result rounded to double at the end. Not reproducible without big rationals | CPU by design |
| Constructive, trivial arithmetic | `CG_Extrude`/`ST_Extrude`, `CG_Translate`, `CG_3DTranslate`, `CG_Scale*`, `CG_Rotate*` (sin/cos), `CG_ForceLHR`, `CG_MakeSolid`, NURBS functions | `x+dx` rounded once equals the IEEE add (if `to_double` rounds to nearest, verify); Rotate uses trigonometry | CPU: the output is a variable-length geometry (the A8/R12 limit) and the work is memory-bound |

**2. How PostGIS calls it.** `lwgeom_sfcgal.c` converts the detoasted geometry to an SFCGAL geometry (every double becomes an exact `Gmpq` via the lazy kernel), runs the algorithm, and converts back with `to_double` (verify). The cost on the CPU is therefore mostly conversion and lazy-node allocation, not the algorithm. Planner hook: the same place the existing slices use. `codegen.c` recognizes a function by oid; for SFCGAL the oids are resolved by name in the schema of the `postgis_sfcgal` extension at planning time (both the `CG_*` function and the `ST_*` wrapper, because the wrapper is a SQL function that the planner may or may not inline), guarded by a `vulkan_sfcgal_*_calibrated(oid)` check that runs fixed vectors through the installed SQL function and compares the device result bit for bit (the pattern of `vulkan_geom_len_calibrated`). The calibration is also the answer to the open details of `to_double` rounding: whatever the installed build does is the definition. The CPU fallback is the original SQL function: nothing to implement (the A12 recheck path re-evaluates the qual per row).

**3. GPU feasibility, honestly.**
- *Scalars (bit-exact, same answer):* feasible. `f64_soft.inc` and the expansion arithmetic of `area_ring.inc` (R3: exact fallback under a cancellation test) already give exact sums of products; the new pieces are an expansion-to-nearest-double conversion (the same rounding as CGAL; calibrated), device `sqrt` (the soft one), and bounded expansion length with the poison-record recheck when it overflows. Volume sums many tetrahedra: the expansion grows, so cap and recheck.
- *`CG_3DIntersects` / `CG_Intersects`:* exact `do_intersect` has many degenerate branches (coplanar triangle pairs). Split: the GPU answers "definitely disjoint" (bounding box, then conservative float interval triangle tests) and the CPU runs SFCGAL on the survivors (recheck, as A12/A13). It never answers "intersects" itself in the first version. In practice users already put `a.geom &&& b.geom` or a GiST join in front, so the GPU only adds the triangle-level culling, which pays only when the per-pair SFCGAL cost is large (meshes of thousands of faces).
- *Constructives:* no. Rational results cannot be reproduced; a "disjoint pair, empty result" shortcut would need the exact empty-geometry representation per function and gains little.
- *Speed:* unmeasured, and the history of this branch is a warning: the PostGIS scans (R1) were 500 ms against 70-250 ms on the CPU because the scan floor dominates. The GPU can win only when the CPU's per-row SFCGAL cost (conversion to exact types, microseconds per vertex, verify) exceeds the per-row upload plus the floor, i.e. big polyhedral surfaces or TINs in many rows, and for many-pair culling. A scan of small buildings will not win. Hence the spike below is a gate, not a formality.

**4. Optionality, build, licensing.** Nothing links SFCGAL or CGAL: the module and the service only resolve function oids by name at planning time and implement the arithmetic themselves; with no `postgis_sfcgal` extension the lookup fails and nothing is offered (as `vulkan_geom_*_calibrated` does when PostGIS is missing); no new `Makefile` switch except possibly `WITH_SFCGAL_TESTS` for the test dependencies (Homebrew `sfcgal`, `cgal`, `gmp`, `mpfr`, `boost` are installed here; I did not verify that the installed `postgis_sfcgal-3` library loads). Licensing, **for legal review, not advice**: SFCGAL's repository `LICENSE` is the GNU Library GPL v2 [web, gitlab.com/sfcgal/SFCGAL/-/raw/master/LICENSE]; CGAL is dual-licensed, with some packages under GPL (the straight skeleton, Nef polyhedra, triangulations and alpha shapes are the ones the constructive functions use; verify per package at cgal.org/license.html). Calling the installed extension through SQL links nothing. Porting code (or translating CGAL's algorithm line by line) is a different question; the plan therefore reimplements only what is mathematics in the public domain (Shewchuk-style expansions, already in the tree from published papers) and no CGAL/SFCGAL code, and the legal review should confirm that before any slice that goes beyond it.

**5. Slices, order, tests.**
| Slice | Content | Effort | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 spike | Time `CG_3DArea`, `CG_Area`, `CG_Volume`, `CG_Orientation`, `CG_IsPlanar`, `CG_3DIntersects` on 1M generated rows (cubes, extruded polygons, TINs of 10 to 10,000 faces) against the Metal scan floor; check the `to_double` rounding and the `ST_` wrapper inlining. **Gate: continue only if a slice projects to 2x or better.** | 2-3 days | none |
| S1 | `CG_3DArea`, `CG_Area`, `CG_Volume` (Z, M, ZM, polyhedral surface, TIN, solid, collections; holes) | 1-2 weeks | the rounding of the exact value; expansion length |
| S2 | `CG_Orientation`, `CG_IsPlanar` (exact signs) | 1 week | degenerate rings, near-planar |
| S3 | `CG_3DIntersects` culling with recheck (scan filter and the A13 join) | 2 weeks | little gain behind a bounding-box filter |
| later | `CG_Distance`/`CG_3DDistance`, only on evidence | | |

Test strategy: `fuzz.py` style differential (GPU path against `pg_strom.enabled=off`) over generated solids and surfaces with edge values (coincident faces, zero-area triangles, collinear and near-planar vertices, huge and tiny coordinates, empty, NaN, Z/M/ZM) plus the calibration guards. Kernel budget: each slice is its own feature group in `scan_prog.feat` and its own tier, scan and aggregate kernels only (the scan kernel with all groups is 6.2 MB; the area group alone cost +6.9 MB in the join kernel, so no SFCGAL code goes into a join kernel before a measurement); stay under the 10.5 MB limit of R15.

**Recommendation.** Worth doing: only S0, then S1 and S2 if the gate passes (a small subset, about 4 weeks), and S3 only if a user has large-mesh intersection queries. Stay on the CPU by design: every constructive function, `CG_IsSolid`, `CG_MakeSolid`, transforms. As a whole milestone it ranks below R5-R7 (predicates and distances of core PostGIS that every spatial query uses): SFCGAL is a niche (3D building/CAD data), its CPU cost is dominated by exact-type conversion, and the GPU gain is uncertain until S0 measures it. Do not start before R1-R15 are done.

Sources: https://postgis.net/docs/reference_sfcgal.html (function list), https://gitlab.com/sfcgal/SFCGAL/-/raw/master/src/algorithm/area.cpp (area3D), https://gitlab.com/sfcgal/SFCGAL/-/raw/master/LICENSE; local: `postgis_sfcgal--3.7.0.sql`, `/opt/homebrew/opt/sfcgal/include/SFCGAL/Kernel.h`, `algorithm/volume.h`.

## A16 status (planned 2026-11-09): GPU mesh booleans for 3D solids, CPU (SFCGAL) as the fallback

**Engine choice (2026-11-14):** the engine is selected by `pg_strom.mesh_boolean_engines` (GPU first, optional Manifold, SFCGAL always last as the oracle); see "A16 engine selection" below.

**Why a separate milestone.** A15 puts scalar measures and pair culling of `postgis_sfcgal` on the GPU and leaves every constructive function on the CPU by design. The user wants the 3D mesh booleans accelerated. The planner-level approach of A11/A13/A15 (replace a function call by a GPU or CPU path) cannot reach inside SFCGAL's boolean implementation, so this needs our own engine, with SFCGAL as the fallback behind the same SQL function. Nothing here is built; this section is the research agenda and the gate.

**Engine sketch (stages, each its own kernel tier; none in the join kernels).**
1. *Broad phase:* triangle pairs of the two solids with overlapping boxes (BVH build and traversal on the device, or a sort-and-sweep). GPU friendly.
2. *Exact narrow phase:* classify each pair (disjoint, crossing, touching, coplanar) with filtered predicates: an interval stage on the software binary64 (directed rounding emulated), then fixed-size Shewchuk expansions for orientation tests (degree 3), with a recheck to the CPU when an expansion would overflow.
3. *Constructions:* intersection points of an input edge with an input face are rational of bounded degree in the input coordinates, so numerator and denominator fit fixed-size big integers (a few hundred bits) and the double can be rounded correctly. This depends on constructions staying ONE level deep (never a point built from constructed points).
4. *Re-triangulation of cut faces:* constrained triangulation on those points (predicates of higher degree). The hardest sequential part; may stay on the CPU in a first version (GPU produces the cut segments per face, CPU triangulates).
5. *Inside/outside classification* of the uncut faces (parallel ray casting or winding number with exact predicates), then selection by operation and assembly of the result mesh and its orientation.

**Fallback rule.** The engine returns "decline" for: coplanar overlapping faces, touching along edges or vertices that the filters cannot decide, non-manifold or non-closed input, expansion overflow, any internal inconsistency (Euler characteristic, orientation), meshes over a size limit. A decline, an error or a lost device re-runs the original SQL function (SFCGAL). The fallback is the oracle.

**Equality definition (to be agreed BEFORE any code).** SFCGAL's output vertex order and triangulation depend on its internals, so a byte-identical WKT is out of reach. Proposal: results are equal when (a) the exact volume and surface area agree to the last bit of the double (computed by the same exact expansions on both outputs), (b) the set of vertices after merging identical points agrees, and (c) the two meshes are the same after canonicalisation (merge coplanar adjacent triangles, sort, normalise orientation). The planner gate `vulkan_sfcgal_bool_calibrated()` runs fixed solids through the installed SFCGAL and the engine and keeps the function on the CPU on any difference, as the other guards do. A user-visible switch `pg_strom.sfcgal_gpu_booleans = off | on` (default off until the engine passes) and a documented statement that the result is geometrically equal but may be triangulated differently.

**Research to do (each item has a way to answer it and an owner-able deliverable).**
| # | Question | How to answer |
|---|---|---|
| R-A | What does the installed SFCGAL use for the 3D booleans: Nef polyhedra, `Polygon_mesh_processing::corefine_and_compute_*`, or its own code, per function and version? Which CGAL kernel is it built with? | Read the SFCGAL sources of the installed version (the Homebrew bottle's version), the `CG_3DUnion` call chain, CGAL release notes; confirm by running with `perf`/`sample` |
| R-B | Where does the CPU time go for typical inputs: broad phase, exact predicates, constructions, triangulation, Nef conversion, WKB conversion? | Profile (`sample`, `perf`, CGAL profiling macros) on the car, plane and house benchmark meshes at 1e4, 1e5 and 1e6 triangles (plus generated spheres, tori and random triangle soups as controls); report the split per size and per object. Amdahl bound for each stage |
| R-C | How clean is real data: share of coplanar or touching faces, non-manifold input, tiny slivers (these decline to the CPU)? | Measure on the car, plane and house benchmark meshes (see Benchmark meshes below); count triangle pairs per category with the exact CPU predicates; include the share of files that are valid solids at all |
| R-D | Can the one-level construction rule hold in corefinement, and what are the degrees and bit sizes of the numerators, denominators and the predicates used by the triangulation step? | Derive on paper for edge-face intersection, then check on generated data with GMP (`mpq`): maximum bit lengths; decide the fixed expansion sizes (must fit registers/shared memory) |
| R-E | Can the device do the exact stage without 64-bit integers or fp64 (Apple GPUs have neither)? | Prototype big-integer add/mul/compare on 32-bit limbs in Slang (the 16-bit-limb technique of the 64-bit sums, `f64_soft.inc`), measure registers, SPIR-V size and Metal compile time |
| R-F | What is the achievable speedup? | Spike S0: the GPU broad phase plus exact narrow phase producing the intersecting-pair set for the generated and real meshes; compare with the CPU time of the same stages (R-B); project the end-to-end time with the CPU doing the rest |
| R-G | Memory and transfer: a 1e6-triangle mesh is tens of MB; the pair list can be large; the result mesh comes back. Does the transfer erase the gain on discrete GPUs (unified memory on Apple does not)? | Model and measure on this Mac; state the discrete-GPU risk (unvalidated platform) |
| R-H | Licensing: SFCGAL LGPL v2, CGAL packages under LGPL or GPL. We call the installed extension and reimplement only public mathematics (Shewchuk-style predicates, BVH). | Legal review before any code is derived from CGAL or SFCGAL sources; keep the engine clean-room from papers |
| R-I | Literature to read before designing: Shewchuk, "Adaptive precision floating-point arithmetic and fast robust geometric predicates"; Lévy, "Exact predicates, exact constructions and combinatorics for mesh CSG" (2022) and the GPU mesh CSG literature; CGAL `Polygon_mesh_processing` corefinement (Loriot et al.); Karras, "Maximizing parallelism in the construction of BVHs"; Zhou et al. "Mesh arrangements for solid geometry" (libigl); Attene "Indirect predicates for geometric constructions" | Summarise per paper: what is exact, what is parallel, what is reusable here; cite URLs |

**Benchmark meshes: real-world objects (decided 2026-11-09: a car, a plane, a house; nothing is started).** The spike uses meshes of real things, not only generated spheres and tori, because the decline rate (R-C) and the share of serial work (R-B) depend on how real surfaces look.
- *Objects and why.* A **car** (CAD-like: many small coplanar and nearly coplanar panels, thin shells, many parts, fillets; stresses the decline path), a **plane** (long thin smooth surfaces, wings meeting a fuselage: a large union with a long curved intersection curve), a **house** (planar faces, axis-aligned walls, openings: the coplanar and touching case that is typical for buildings, and the one the fallback must handle without surprising cost).
- *Sizes.* Each at three resolutions, about 1e4, 1e5 and 1e6 triangles (decimate or remesh a high-resolution source with a fixed tool and record its version), so the speedup is measured as a function of size.
- *Where to get them (to evaluate; check the licence of each BEFORE a file enters the repository or the benchmark data; keep the files outside the repository under `test-data/meshes/` with a manifest of source, licence, size and checksum).* Public collections of 3D and CAD models (for example the ShapeNet / ModelNet categories car and airplane, Thingi10K, the ABC CAD dataset, Objaverse, Google Scanned Objects, the Stanford 3D scanning repository), and open building data for houses (3D building models in CityGML LoD1/LoD2 such as 3DBAG, OpenStreetMap building parts, IFC sample models). Many are non-commercial or share-alike: record the licence per file; if none is acceptable for redistribution, only the generator scripts and the manifest go into the repository.
- *Condition of real meshes (a research finding in itself).* Real models are often NOT valid solids: open boundaries, non-manifold edges, duplicate vertices, self-intersections, inconsistent orientation, multiple shells. The 3D booleans need closed, consistently oriented solids. Report, per object, the fraction of files that SFCGAL accepts as a solid (`CG_IsValid`, `ST_IsSolid`), and what repair is needed (merge duplicate vertices, orient, fill holes, remove self-intersections; with which tool, and whether the repair changes the geometry). The benchmark uses the repaired solid and the manifest records the repair steps; a mesh the engine would have to repair itself is out of scope (it declines).
- *Boolean scenarios (each run with SFCGAL on the CPU first, for R-B and R-C).* car minus a box (a cut: a large, clean intersection), car intersect a box, car union the same car translated by a small offset (heavy overlap and near-coincident surfaces: the worst case for degeneracy), plane union fuselage and wings built as separate solids, plane minus a cylinder (an engine bore), house union an extension (touching and coplanar walls), house minus window and door boxes (coplanar faces at the cut), and a many-pairs batch (a table of 1,000 small houses against one site volume: the many-pairs case where a GPU could win).
- *Metrics.* CPU time per operation split by stage (R-B), triangles in the intersecting region versus the whole mesh, decline rate per category (R-C), peak memory, and for the spike the GPU time of the pair-set stage and the projected end-to-end speedup, per object and size. The gate (3x projected, under 20% decline) is applied per object class and reported per class: it is acceptable for one class to pass and another to fail (the plane may pass where the house declines too often), and the milestone then scopes itself to the classes that pass.

**Slices and gates.**
- *S0 (spike, about 2 weeks):* R-A, R-B, R-C, R-E, R-F. **Gate 1:** continue only if the projected end-to-end speedup is 3x or more at the target mesh sizes and the decline rate on the real data is under 20%.
- *S1:* broad phase plus exact classification as a standalone, also exposed as an accelerated `CG_3DIntersects` for solids (a predicate, no construction; useful by itself).
- *S2:* exact one-level constructions and the cut segments per face; CPU triangulation. **Gate 2:** correct results against SFCGAL on 10,000 generated pairs incl. degenerate cases and a measured speedup.
- *S3:* GPU classification and assembly; `CG_3DIntersection`, then union and difference. Equality tests per the definition above; fuzzing as the A6/A9 fuzzer does, including self-touching and nearly coplanar inputs.
- *S4 (optional):* GPU constrained triangulation.

**Effort and risk.** Research-grade; S0 about 2 weeks, S1-S3 about 3-4 months, S4 open. The main risk is that the serial parts (triangulation, bookkeeping) dominate and the GPU stage is a small fraction of the time (then the milestone stops at S1). Second risk: coplanar and touching data is common and declines too often. Third: the Metal compile budget for fixed-size big-integer kernels (each stage its own tier, below about 10.5 MB, see R15). Ranks after R1-R15 and A15 S0-S2.


## A16 engine selection: GPU first, Manifold and SFCGAL as selectable CPU engines (planned 2026-11-14, rewritten the same day; nothing is built or run)

**Decision of the user (2026-11-14).** Do not replace SFCGAL; make the mesh-boolean engine a configuration choice, with the GPU first and the CPU as the fallback (the standing rule of the branch). Manifold (`elalish/manifold`; Homebrew formula `manifold` 3.5.4; Apache-2.0 as far as I know, to be read from the source in G1) and SFCGAL are both optional. The investigation below measures whether each engine can be offered at all and what Manifold can contribute to the GPU engine.

**The engines.**
| Engine | What it is | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `gpu` | The own A16 engine on the Vulkan device (broad phase, exact narrow phase, constructions, classification; stages in "A16 status"). Ideas, and with Apache-2.0 possibly code, from Manifold (gate G5); never CGAL-derived code (R-H). | First choice once it passes its guard. Declines (coplanar, touching, non-manifold, overflow, size limit) or errors go to the next engine. |
| `manifold` | Manifold linked as an optional CPU library (parallel on the CPU). | An opt-in CPU engine between the GPU and SFCGAL. Offered only if G2 (agreement) and G3 (degenerate input) pass and G4 (speed) pays. Results are "geometrically equal to SFCGAL's, not identical". |
| `sfcgal` | The installed `postgis_sfcgal` function, called through SQL as today. | The oracle and the last fallback. Always implicitly last in the chain: a chain can never end without it, and `pg_strom.enabled = off` always means SFCGAL. |

**The setting.** `pg_strom.mesh_boolean_engines`, an ordered list, default `'gpu'` (so the chain is gpu, then sfcgal). Examples: `'sfcgal'` (always the oracle), `'gpu,manifold'` (GPU, then Manifold, then SFCGAL), `'manifold'`. Each engine is tried in order; a decline, an error, a lost device, a missing library or a failed calibration guard moves on to the next, and SFCGAL runs last. An engine that is not built or installed is skipped with a DEBUG message, not an error. Until the GPU engine passes its gates the default chain behaves as `'sfcgal'` (the planner does not offer the engine). The planner swaps the function call as A15 does, so `CG_3DUnion(...)` is unchanged in SQL, in tests and in dumps. A guard per engine (`vulkan_sfcgal_bool_calibrated()` generalized: fixed solids through the engine and the installed SFCGAL, keep the engine off on any difference under the equality definition of A16) runs at planning time.

**Consequences to document.** Engines can return different but equally valid triangulations of the same boolean, so the setting affects stored results (materialized views, indexes on results, replicas, dumps restored under another setting). The default must stay conservative, the documentation must say it, and each engine needs its own differential and calibration tests against SFCGAL.
