The module must keep building and running with no SFCGAL and no Manifold installed: Manifold is an optional build dependency (like `WITH_GPUSERV=auto|1|0`, for example `WITH_MANIFOLD=auto|1|0`), dlopen or a separate module if linking it would pull in a heavy runtime (to be decided in M0), and it goes into the README credits table the moment it is built against.

**SFCGAL is never a candidate that can lose.** `CG_3DUnion`, `CG_3DIntersection` and `CG_3DDifference` run SFCGAL whenever the GPU and the other engines are off or decline, and every differential test of this branch takes SFCGAL's answer as the truth ("the GPU result equals the CPU result"). The investigation only decides what else is offered in front of it.

**Gates, tightest constraint first; each gate can stop an engine and the stop is recorded here.**
| Gate | Question | How it is answered | Stops |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 licences | What exactly are the licences of Manifold 3.5.4 and of its dependencies (Homebrew formula deps, for example Clipper2 and a threading library), of the installed SFCGAL, and of the installed CGAL packages `Nef_3`, `Polygon_mesh_processing` and the kernel? | Read the `LICENSE` / `COPYING` files in the source trees and the CGAL per-package `package_info/*/license.txt`; quote file paths; fold the result into R-H and the Licensing section | the `manifold` engine, and any port of Manifold code, if a dependency or the library is copyleft in a way that conflicts with the PostgreSQL License |
| G2 agreement with SFCGAL | Does Manifold's output equal SFCGAL's under the equality definition of A16 (exact volume and area, vertex set after merging, canonical mesh)? First read in the Manifold source what coordinate type the intersection points are computed in (float or double) and whether constructed vertices are rounded: single precision or a different rounding rules out agreement whatever the topology | M0 harness below; plus R-A (which CGAL calls SFCGAL makes per function) to interpret differences | the `manifold` engine, if agreement is not reachable on clean generated solids (reading it for the GPU engine can continue) |
| G3 degenerate input | Coplanar overlap, shared edges, shared vertices, slivers (the house case). Manifold resolves ties by symbolic perturbation, SFCGAL exactly: how often does each decline, error, or return a different topology? | M1 below, counted per category | the `manifold` engine, if its answers on degenerate input differ from SFCGAL's silently (a wrong answer is worse than a decline); it could stay offered with a decline-on-degenerate pre-check (a CPU exact predicate) if that is cheap and complete |
| G4 speed | Where does Manifold beat SFCGAL, single thread and parallel, at 1e4, 1e5 and 1e6 triangles? Timed through SQL (including WKB conversion) and library-direct, so the conversion is counted separately | M2 below, only if G2 and G3 leave the engine alive | the `manifold` engine, if the gain is under 3x at the target sizes (the A16 gate 1 figure) |
| G5 GPU portability and reuse | Does Manifold ship a GPU backend or only TBB / parallel-STL? Do its stages need 64-bit integers or fp64 (Apple GPUs have neither; same question as R-E)? What would a port to Slang cost per stage, and which of its stages could replace the plan's own sketch? | M3 below (reading, no code) | Manifold as a source for the `gpu` engine (the own sketch continues) |

**Proofs of concept (CPU only, small, in `spike/a16-manifold/`; data under `test-data/meshes/` with the manifest the plan already requires; everything at `nice -n 10`, because ../mongo benchmarks on this machine; each has a stop rule).**
- **M0 harness.** Link Homebrew Manifold; run union, intersection and difference of generated solids (offset cubes, spheres, tori, translated copies) and run the same inputs through `postgis_sfcgal` in a scratch cluster (first check that the installed PostGIS provides the extension: `select name from pg_available_extensions where name = 'postgis_sfcgal'` in a cluster of its own, port from `test-data/agent_common_rules.txt`). Also decide linking against dlopen and the size of the runtime. Compute exact volume and area of both outputs with GMP `mpq`, compare per the equality definition. Stop the `manifold` engine if the two disagree beyond rounding on the clean inputs.
- **M1 degenerate set.** Coplanar overlapping faces, a shared edge, a shared vertex, nearly coplanar slivers; per category: result, decline, error, topology difference. Stop if Manifold is silently wrong on a category that SFCGAL handles.
- **M2 timing.** Only if G2 and G3 leave the engine alive. Single thread and all threads, library-direct and through SQL, on generated meshes and the car, plane and house meshes of the benchmark section (after their licences are checked).
- **M3 reading, no code.** Map Manifold's boolean pipeline onto the five engine stages of A16 and mark each as data-parallel or serial; cite the algorithm's source paper from Manifold's own documentation rather than from memory. This feeds R-I and G5.

**Advisor checkpoints (the advisor sees the whole conversation; show it tables, not summaries).**
1. *Before M0:* the input meshes and the metrics (what exactly is compared, the tolerance for "equal", the sizes).
2. *After M0 and M1:* the decision which engines are offered at all: the agreement table (G2) and the degenerate-category table (G3) per operation.
3. *Before any go / no-go on A16 itself (gate 1 of S0):* G4 and G5 numbers plus the A16 research items R-B, R-C, R-F.

**Housekeeping rules.** If Manifold is linked or built, even for a proof of concept, it goes into the README credits table with its licence. R-A (what SFCGAL calls) is folded into the G2 setup. The `doc/` page for the engine setting is written with the first engine that is offered.

**Status.** Planned; the user has not yet asked to start M0, and A16 was put on hold on 2026-11-09 ("wait"). Ask before running any proof of concept.

## Licensing (decided 2026-11-11)
`kernels_vm/area_ring.inc` reproduces PostGIS 3.7's `ptarray_signed_area` (GPL-2.0-or-later). Decision by the user: accept GPL for that file. It carries an SPDX header and `LICENSES/GPL-2.0.txt` is in the tree; binaries of `pgstrom-gpuserv` that include it are distributed under GPL terms (packaging in `files/` must ship the license and a source offer: not done yet). Re-check the README credits table whenever a dependency or reproduced algorithm is added.

## A17 discrete-GPU performance (T4 benchmark, 2026-11-14)
**Finding (CI run 43573554810, hosted T4, `perf_bench.sh`, 16.8M rows, one run, PostgreSQL side with one worker per runner core):** every query is correct but the GPU path is 2.5x to 4x *slower* than parallel PostgreSQL end to end (no-GROUP-BY aggregate 1,389 ms vs 434 ms; GROUP BY 100 keys 2,488 vs 849; aggregate over a join 1,533 vs 329) and GROUP BY 20 over a join takes 7,280 ms vs 877 with no task trace lines (0 tasks: a CPU path or a cold kernel; unknown). The kernel time is small (10 ms per task, 48 tasks = 0.5 s of 1.4 s). The first run of each query is the slowest (1.6 s, 2.4 s: cold T4 clocks, see the mongo finding that idle clocks slow the first query). Fixed already: read-back buffers in the cached memory type (4 MB/s -> 11.9 GB/s, bdde8c1a).
**Plan:** (1) attribute the 0.9 s per query: the second benchmark run uploads the service log with per-task trace lines (pages, bytes, kernel time) and prints nproc and the GPU clocks; (2) the likely costs are the heap-page copy by the backend, the upload over PCIe (the M1 maps the file without a copy), per-task submit/wait, and idle clocks; candidates: larger batches, pinned staging buffers, overlapped upload and compute (double buffering), reading pages straight from shared buffers into a mapped host-visible buffer; (3) find why the join GROUP BY 20 shows 0 tasks; (4) re-run on the T4 after each change, and keep the doc/features.md T4 table honest.
